# Asparagus

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="asparagus-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="asparagus-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="asparagus-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Asparagus in the HMI library has 78 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, tin, uranium, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn, Se, I, Mo and Sr from 10 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Norton GJ, Deacon CM, Mestrot A, Feldmann J, Jenkins P, Baskaran C, Meharg AA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Cadmium and lead in vegetable and fruit produce selected from specific regional areas of the UK</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">The Science of the Total Environment 533, 520-527 (2015); and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Joint FAO/WHO Expert Committee on Food Additives · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Cadmium: dietary exposure assessment</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">WHO Food Additives Series, No. 82 (Safety evaluation of certain contaminants in food, prepared by the 91st meeting of JECFA)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

<ul>
<li>
<strong>Metals measured:</strong>

Lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury and nickel and others, in 78 results from 10 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Norton GJ, Deacon CM, Mestrot A, Feldmann J, Jenkins P, Baskaran C, Meharg AA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Cadmium and lead in vegetable and fruit produce selected from specific regional areas of the UK</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">The Science of the Total Environment 533, 520-527 (2015); accepted author manuscript</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/chigona2026-great-dyke-wild-foods-geophagy|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Chigona A, Mapanda F, Benhura C, Nyamangara J, Nyamugafata P, Wuta M, Nondo N, Mavunga S, Nota T · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/chigona2026-great-dyke-wild-foods-geophagy">Human Health Risks Associated with the Consumption of Wild Foods and Geophagic Material Along the Great Dyke and Surrounding Areas in Zimbabwe</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">International Journal of Environmental Research and Public Health 2026, 23, 957</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/chigona2026-great-dyke-wild-foods-geophagy">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Main drivers:</strong>

Form, basis and where the asparagus was sampled; reporting limits decide how many results are non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Norton GJ, Deacon CM, Mestrot A, Feldmann J, Jenkins P, Baskaran C, Meharg AA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Cadmium and lead in vegetable and fruit produce selected from specific regional areas of the UK</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">The Science of the Total Environment 533, 520-527 (2015); accepted author manuscript</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/chigona2026-great-dyke-wild-foods-geophagy|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Chigona A, Mapanda F, Benhura C, Nyamangara J, Nyamugafata P, Wuta M, Nondo N, Mavunga S, Nota T · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/chigona2026-great-dyke-wild-foods-geophagy">Human Health Risks Associated with the Consumption of Wild Foods and Geophagic Material Along the Great Dyke and Surrounding Areas in Zimbabwe</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">International Journal of Environmental Research and Public Health 2026, 23, 957</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/chigona2026-great-dyke-wild-foods-geophagy">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Practical framing:</strong>

Ask for lot results with a quantification limit well below the limit that applies in the destination market.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Norton GJ, Deacon CM, Mestrot A, Feldmann J, Jenkins P, Baskaran C, Meharg AA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Cadmium and lead in vegetable and fruit produce selected from specific regional areas of the UK</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">The Science of the Total Environment 533, 520-527 (2015); accepted author manuscript</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/chigona2026-great-dyke-wild-foods-geophagy|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Chigona A, Mapanda F, Benhura C, Nyamangara J, Nyamugafata P, Wuta M, Nondo N, Mavunga S, Nota T · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/chigona2026-great-dyke-wild-foods-geophagy">Human Health Risks Associated with the Consumption of Wild Foods and Geophagic Material Along the Great Dyke and Surrounding Areas in Zimbabwe</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">International Journal of Environmental Research and Public Health 2026, 23, 957</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/chigona2026-great-dyke-wild-foods-geophagy">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · from 1,147 results in 4 reporting regions, with subgroup medians from 2 to 1,110 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> In the US Total Diet Study, asparagus, cooked had a median lead result below 4 µg/kg across 27 composites from 2017–2020.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Across 2 other studies of ordinary farms and markets, reported lead means and medians on an as-sold or fresh-weight basis ranged from 2.2 µg/kg (Spungen et al. 2024) to 5.2 µg/kg (Norton et al. 2015).<sup class="cite">[[sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Norton GJ, Deacon CM, Mestrot A, Feldmann J, Jenkins P, Baskaran C, Meharg AA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Cadmium and lead in vegetable and fruit produce selected from specific regional areas of the UK</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">The Science of the Total Environment 533, 520-527 (2015); accepted author manuscript</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Joint FAO/WHO Expert Committee on Food Additives 2022 reported cadmium at 11.4 µg/kg (mean) in asparagus, as sold from GEMS/Food contaminants database.<sup class="cite">[[sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Joint FAO/WHO Expert Committee on Food Additives · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Cadmium: dietary exposure assessment</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">WHO Food Additives Series, No. 82 (Safety evaluation of certain contaminants in food, prepared by the 91st meeting of JECFA)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the asparagus came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.



<a id="ingredient-identity-and-scope"></a>
## What counts as asparagus

<a id="hmi-ing-001"></a>
<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-002"></a>
<a id="which-processing-states-and-derivatives-are-distinct-materials-rather-than-synonyms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-003"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-can-be-transferred-between-those-materials-and-what-requires-a-separate-answer"></a>

This page covers asparagus in 3 forms kept separate in every comparison: asparagus, as sold (68 results), asparagus, cooked (9 results) and asparagus, canned (1 result).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Norton GJ, Deacon CM, Mestrot A, Feldmann J, Jenkins P, Baskaran C, Meharg AA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Cadmium and lead in vegetable and fruit produce selected from specific regional areas of the UK</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">The Science of the Total Environment 533, 520-527 (2015); Results stay on the basis each source reports: as sold or fresh weight, dry weight, or unstated. Dry-weight, as-sold and unstated results are never pooled, and no moisture conversion is applied.

8 rows can be rated against an HMI reference for the same form and basis, 3 rows have a direct same-study comparison, and the rest stay unrated with the reason recorded.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-asparagus" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-asparagus-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of asparagus, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤5</td><td>≤7</td><td>≤10</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤5</td><td>≤7</td><td>≤9.63</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>61 reference units; 1 sampling family. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>61</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for asparagus, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>WHO GEMS/Food is the only compatible sampling family for asparagus, as sold on this basis in the completed asparagus ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Ventura et al. 2025</td><td>1</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for asparagus, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>Ventura et al. 2025 is the only compatible sampling family for asparagus, as sold on this basis in the completed asparagus ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/ventura2025-portuguese-total-diet-study-trace-elements" class="internal" data-slug="sources/ventura2025-portuguese-total-diet-study-trace-elements" data-no-cite-meta>Ventura et al. 2025</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Method: hmi-pooled-reference-v2.</p><p>hmi-pooled-reference-v2:asparagus-38c77f94e833:513850a289cc</p><p>The reference is computed by HMI from admitted evidence, not copied from a study baseline. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div></details><div hidden data-form-reasons><p data-reason="no_numeric_value_or_bound" data-title="Spread or extreme value only">The source reports only a percentile, range or maximum for these samples, with no mean or median to place against the reference. Spread figures that do have a mean or median are shown on that row instead.</p><p data-reason="bounds_span_categories" data-title="Non-detect spans the bands">The result is below a reporting limit, and the range from zero to that limit crosses more than one band.</p><p data-reason="species_or_material_without_compatible_reference_after_full_ledger" data-title="No reference for this form">No medians or sample values exist for this form and metal, so no HMI reference could be built.</p><p data-reason="reference_below_display_minimum_after_full_ledger" data-title="Reference too small">The HMI reference for this form has fewer than four units or no measurable spread, below the display minimum.</p><p data-reason="basis_incompatible_with_all_references" data-title="Basis has no reference">The result is on a basis (dry weight, or not stated) that no HMI reference for this form uses, so it cannot be placed.</p><p data-reason="bounded" data-title="Limits overlap">Reporting limits overlap the study comparison, so no direction can be shown.</p></div><script type="application/octet-stream" data-explorer-payload data-evidence-content><p class="hmi-dataset-title"><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">WHO GEMS/Food</a></p><p>Asparagus, as sold · Lead (Pb) · 748 results.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Selected finding and reference</p><div class="hmi-selected-result" data-selected-finding><strong class="hmi-finding">median &lt;6 µg/kg (below reporting limits)</strong><span class="hmi-row-note">median.</span><p>748 results. Basis: As sold, as eaten or fresh weight.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p></div><div class="hmi-selected-comparison" data-selected-comparison><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">HMI baseline position</p><p>Limited data · HMI asparagus Pb reference · Asparagus, as sold. median as reported: ≤6 µg/kg. HMI median ≤7; Q1 ≤5; Q3 ≤10 µg/kg. 1 family. Reporting bounds prevent a rating.</p><p>Reporting bounds or insufficient reference spread prevent a stable Low / Moderate / High classification.</p><p>This finding’s sampling family contributes to the fixed reference.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to weighting</p><p>Equal weight per subgroup: Limited data. The position remains unresolved under this alternative. This check does not address all sampling, rounding or analytical uncertainty.</p><div data-baseline-reference="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:asparagus-cec62f461abc:978b0332f576"><a href="#hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:asparagus-cec62f461abc:978b0332f576">Reference, contributing evidence and methods</a></div></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>2001–2019</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>EURO</p><div data-selected-regional><p>Purchase or reporting market; growing area not identified.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>As described by the monitoring programme.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p><p></p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Shared samples and study overlap</p><p>Subgroup rows from the same source share its laboratory and sampling frame; they are not independent studies.</p><p>Each table row describes a food/analyte result. This check does not address all sampling, rounding or analytical uncertainty.</p><div data-baseline-reference="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:asparagus-38c77f94e833:513850a289cc"><a href="#hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:asparagus-38c77f94e833:513850a289cc">Reference, contributing evidence and methods</a></div></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>2014–2016</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>pooled TDS sample</p><div data-selected-regional><p>All samples in the study.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>ICP-MS after microwave HNO3/H2O2 digestion (I by TMAH alkaline extraction).</p><p>No censoring reported</p><p>Pooled TDS sample (triplicate mean).</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Shared samples and study overlap</p><p>Subgroup rows from the same source share its laboratory and sampling frame; they are not independent studies.</p><p>Each table row describes a food/analyte result. Repeated rows do not add independent samples or datasets. ## Mitigation for asparagus

<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>

<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing asparagus for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>

<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 2 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 3,000 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a Every source record that names asparagus was decided on its content: 10 occurrence extracted, 10 records in all. The occurrence table renders every extracted result; held values remain visible with their reason.

References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Asparagus FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-asparagus" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-asparagus" data-question="What heavy metals are found in asparagus?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in asparagus?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, tin, uranium, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn, Se, I, Mo and Sr in asparagus, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Norton GJ, Deacon CM, Mestrot A, Feldmann J, Jenkins P, Baskaran C, Meharg AA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Cadmium and lead in vegetable and fruit produce selected from specific regional areas of the UK</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">The Science of the Total Environment 533, 520-527 (2015); accepted author manuscript</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/chigona2026-great-dyke-wild-foods-geophagy|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Chigona A, Mapanda F, Benhura C, Nyamangara J, Nyamugafata P, Wuta M, Nondo N, Mavunga S, Nota T · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/chigona2026-great-dyke-wild-foods-geophagy">Human Health Risks Associated with the Consumption of Wild Foods and Geophagic Material Along the Great Dyke and Surrounding Areas in Zimbabwe</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">International Journal of Environmental Research and Public Health 2026, 23, 957</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/chigona2026-great-dyke-wild-foods-geophagy">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in asparagus, as sold from 1,147 results in 4 reporting regions, with subgroup medians from 2 to 1,110 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-asparagus" data-anchor="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-asparagus" data-question="Which regions report the lowest levels in asparagus?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which regions report the lowest levels in asparagus?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, asparagus, as sold had the lowest lead subgroup median in SEARO (2 µg/kg) and the highest in EURO (1,110 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium, the lowest regional median was in WPRO (6 µg/kg) and the highest in EURO (45 µg/kg).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-asparagus-is-inorganic" data-anchor="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-asparagus-is-inorganic" data-question="How much of the arsenic in asparagus is inorganic?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How much of the arsenic in asparagus is inorganic?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Inorganic arsenic, the more toxic form, was measured in 4 results from 2 sources, far fewer than total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs|7]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Dietary exposure to inorganic arsenic in the European population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2014;12(3):3597</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) 2014 reported inorganic arsenic at 3.1 µg/kg (mean) in asparagus, as sold from European dietary-survey occurrence data.<sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs|7]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Dietary exposure to inorganic arsenic in the European population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2014;12(3):3597</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
